<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a countable uniformly discrete metric space whose real Lipschitz-free space has the approximation property but fails the bounded approximation property, answering Kalton's question negatively. The identity can be approximated on every compact set by finite-rank operators, but no uniform bound on their norms is possible.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a countable, unbounded, nonproper pointed metric space $(M,d,o)$ such that $d(x,y)\ge1$ for distinct points and $\mathcal F(M)$ has AP but fails $\lambda$-BAP for every finite $\lambda\ge1$.
  - Explanation: The metric wedge has compact-set finite-rank approximations without any uniform operator norm bound.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-blocks)): For each integer $p\ge1$, there are a bounded countably infinite pointed metric space $(M_p,d_p,o)$ and a finite set $A_p\subseteq M_p$ containing $o$ such that $$d_p(x,y)\ge1\quad(x\ne y),\qquad \operatorname{diam}M_p\le B_p:=300p^2+150p+5,$$ and every bounded finite-rank linear operator $T_0:\mathcal F(M_p)\to\mathcal F(M_p)$ with $\left\\|T_0\right\\|\le p$ satisfies $$\max_{a\in A_p}\left\\|T_0\delta_a-\delta_a\right\\|\ge\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: An infinite bounded block has a finite anchor set that obstructs every finite-rank operator within the prescribed norm bound.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-renorming)): For each integer $p\ge1$, real $\ell_1$ has an equivalent norm $\left\\|\cdot\right\\|_p$ such that $$\tfrac12\left\\|c\right\\|_1\le\left\\|c\right\\|_p\le B_p\left\\|c\right\\|_1,$$ and $(\ell_1,\left\\|\cdot\right\\|_p)$ has $2B_p$-BAP but fails $p$-BAP. For $p=1$, this norm fails MAP.
  - Explanation: The free-space norm transfers to an equivalent real sequence-space norm with bounded approximation but a prescribed obstruction.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-coefficients)): Let $(N,d,o)$ be a countable pointed metric space such that $d(x,y)\ge1$ for distinct points and $\operatorname{diam}N\le B<\infty$. The map $$J:\ell_1(N\setminus\{o\})\longrightarrow\mathcal F(N), \qquad Jc=\sum_{x\ne o}c_x\delta_x,$$ is a linear isomorphism onto $\mathcal F(N)$ and satisfies $$\label{eq:coefficient-bounds} \tfrac12\left\\|c\right\\|_1\le\left\\|Jc\right\\|\le B\left\\|c\right\\|_1.$$ If $N\ne\{o\}$, then $\mathcal F(N)$ has $2B$-BAP. If $N=\{o\}$, both spaces are zero.
  - Explanation: The coefficient map identifies a bounded separated free space with absolutely summable coefficients and controls coordinate truncations.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-limit)): For every even integer $\Delta\ge4$ and positive integer $k$, there is a deterministic sequence of nonempty finite $\Delta$-regular multigraphs $G_m$, with vertices colored by $\{1,\ldots,k\}$, such that, for every fixed integer $r\ge0$ and every possible induced rooted colored radius-$r$ type $\tau$, the fraction of vertices of $G_m$ whose ball has type $\tau$ converges to the probability of $\tau$ for the $\Delta$-regular tree with independent uniform vertex colors.
  - Explanation: Independent permutations and vertex colors admit a deterministic sequence with the required colored tree local limit.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-block-geometry)): The preceding construction has the following properties.* 1. *$(M_p,d_p,o)$ is a countably infinite pointed metric space with separation at least one and diameter at most $B=B_p$. The anchor set $A_p$ is finite and contains $o$. Each source edge has endpoints at $d_p$-distance at most one.* 2. *The label map $h:M_p\to\{0,\ldots,D\}^k$ is 1-Lipschitz for the sup norm. If a source vertex $x$ has color $i$, then $h_i(x)=0$, and $h(a(x))=h(x)$.* 3. *For every source vertex $x$ and every anchor $a$, $$d_p(x,a)\ge R=R_p,\qquad d_p(x,a(x))=R.$$ In particular, $d_p(x,o)\ge R_p=30p$.* 4. *Every closed $d_p$-ball of radius $2L$ has at most $Q$ points. For every $t\in M_p$, at most $H$ coordinates of $h(t)$ are strictly below $D$.
  - Explanation: The weighted metric construction preserves separation, bounded diameter, label control, anchor distances and small-ball bounds.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-support)): Suppose that a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T_0:\mathcal F(M_p)\to\mathcal F(M_p)$ satisfies $\left\\|T_0\right\\|\le p$ and $$\left\\|T_0\delta_a-\delta_a\right\\|<\frac12\qquad(a\in A_p).$$ Then there are a finite set $S\subseteq M_p$ containing $o$ and a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T:\mathcal F(M_p)\to\mathcal F(M_p)$ such that $$\label{eq:operator-bounds} \begin{gathered} T\mathcal F(M_p)\subseteq\operatorname{span}\{\delta_j:j\in S\},\qquad \left\\|T\right\\|\le2p,\\ \left\\|T\delta_a-\delta_a\right\\|<1\qquad(a\in A_p). \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: A finite-rank operator can be perturbed to have a common finite evaluation support while retaining the required bounds.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-measure-bounds)): Let $S\subseteq M_p$ be finite and contain $o$, and let $T:\mathcal F(M_p)\to\mathcal F(M_p)$ be a bounded linear operator satisfying <a href="#eq:operator-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:operator-bounds">[eq:operator-bounds]</a>. There is a family $(\mu_x)_{x\in M_p}$ of signed measures on $S$, each of total mass one, such that $$\label{eq:affine-pairing} \int g\,d\mu_x=(T\delta_x)(g-g(o))+g(o)$$ for every real Lipschitz function $g$ on $M_p$. They satisfy $$\label{eq:tv-bound} \left\\|\mu_x\right\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le V=1+4pB\qquad(x\in M_p),$$ and, for every source vertex $x$, $$\label{eq:bump-mass} \int b_x\,d\mu_x\ge\frac{3L}{4}.$$
  - Explanation: Affine signed measures of total mass one encode the supported operator, with variation and bump-integral bounds.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rare-walks)): For a fixed integer $n\ge1$, let $\beta_m$ be the fraction of vertices of $G_m$ from which a matched walk of length $n$ starts. Then $$\label{eq:bad-fraction} \limsup_{m\to\infty}\beta_m \le \|S\|\frac Hk\left(\frac{\Delta QH}{k}\right)^n.$$ Consequently one can choose $n\ge2$ and then $m$ so that $\beta_m<1/(20V)$.
  - Explanation: The tree color estimate bounds the asymptotic fraction of roots admitting long matched walks.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-rem-non-tree-bad-set)): One may enlarge the set counted by $\beta_m$ to include every root whose induced radius-$(n+1)$ neighborhood is not a tree. For fixed $n$, let $\gamma_m$ be the fraction of roots with such a neighborhood and $\widetilde\beta_m$ the enlarged fraction. Then $\beta_m\le\widetilde\beta_m\le\beta_m+\gamma_m$, while the local limit gives $\gamma_m\to0$. Thus <a href="#eq:bad-fraction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bad-fraction">[eq:bad-fraction]</a> remains valid with $\widetilde\beta_m$ in place of $\beta_m$, and the same successive choices of $n\ge2$ and sufficiently large $m$ give $\widetilde\beta_m<1/(20V)$.
  - Explanation: Roots with non-tree neighborhoods may be added to the bad set without changing the limiting estimate.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exceptional-directions)): Let $e:x\to y$ be an incidence of $G$ and let $j,j'\in S$ be states. If $(x,j)$ and $(y,j')$ are compatible and $d_p(j,j')\le2L$, then $j\in E_e$ or $j'\in E_{\bar e}$.
  - Explanation: Compatible nearby states across an incidence force at least one exceptional direction.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-test)): For every incidence $e:x\to y$ of $G$, there is a $1$-Lipschitz function $f_e:M_p\to[-L,L]$ such that $$f_e(j)=b_x(j)\quad(j\in S\setminus E_e),\qquad f_e(j')=-b_y(j')\quad(j'\in S\setminus E_{\bar e}).$$
  - Explanation: Opposing bump prescriptions extend to a bounded scalar Lipschitz test.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rem-abstract-obstruction)): The preceding proof gives the same finite-anchor obstruction for any data satisfying the following assumptions. Fix an integer $p\ge1$ and retain the parameter choices $L,R,K,D,B,V,\Delta,Q,H,k$ of [sec:blocks](#sec:blocks), including <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>, and the incidence convention of that section. 1. $(N,d,o)$ is a countable pointed metric space of separation at least one and diameter at most $B$, and $A\subseteq N$ is finite and contains $o$. 2. The $G_m$ are nonempty finite $\Delta$-regular multigraphs with disjoint vertex sets in $N$ and colors in $\{1,\ldots,k\}$. Edge endpoints have $d$-distance at most one. For every fixed integer $r\ge0$, the empirical distributions of their induced rooted radius-$r$ colored balls converge to the corresponding distribution on the $\Delta$-regular tree with independent uniform colors, as in [lem:local-limit](#lem:local-limit). 3. The map $h:N\to\{0,\ldots,D\}^k$ is $1$-Lipschitz for the sup norm. Every graph vertex $x$ of color $i$ satisfies $h_i(x)=0$ and has an anchor $a(x)\in A$ with $h(a(x))=h(x)$ and $d(x,a(x))\le R$. 4. Every closed $d$-ball of radius $2L$ has at most $Q$ points, and for every $t\in N$ at most $H$ coordinates of $h(t)$ are strictly below $D$. If these data are fixed before $T_0$, every bounded finite-rank real linear operator $T_0:\mathcal F(N)\to\mathcal F(N)$ with $\left\\|T_0\right\\|\le p$ satisfies $$\max_{a\in A}\left\\|T_0\delta_a-\delta_a\right\\|\ge\frac12.$$ Indeed, the support perturbation and signed-measure estimates use only the stated metric, anchor, and label bounds, while the rare-walk, continuation, scalar-test, and averaging steps use only those bounds, the stated graph limit, and the incidence convention.
  - Explanation: The anchor obstruction depends only on the listed metric, label, local-limit and incidence assumptions fixed before the operator.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(N,d,o)$ be a countable pointed metric space such that $d(x,y)\\ge1$ for distinct points and $\\operatorname{diam}N\\le B<\\infty$. The map $$J:\\ell_1(N\\setminus\\{o\\})\\longrightarrow\\mathcal F(N),\n \\qquad Jc=\\sum_{x\\ne o}c_x\\delta_x,$$ is a linear isomorphism onto $\\mathcal F(N)$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:coefficient-bounds}\n \\tfrac12\\left\\|c\\right\\|_1\\le\\left\\|Jc\\right\\|\\le B\\left\\|c\\right\\|_1.$$ If $N\\ne\\{o\\}$, then $\\mathcal F(N)$ has $2B$-BAP. If $N=\\{o\\}$, both spaces are zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-330.1"
      },

If $N=\\{o\\}$, both spaces are zero.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-limit",
        "exact_quote": "For every even integer $\\Delta\\ge4$ and positive integer $k$, there is a deterministic sequence of nonempty finite $\\Delta$-regular multigraphs $G_m$, with vertices colored by $\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, such that, for every fixed integer $r\\ge0$ and every possible induced rooted colored radius-$r$ type $\\tau$, the fraction of vertices of $G_m$ whose ball has type $\\tau$ converges to the probability of $\\tau$ for the $\\Delta$-regular tree with independent uniform vertex colors.",
        "source_id": "PM-330.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-limit",

          "source_id": "PM-330.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-block-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "The preceding construction has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$(M_p,d_p,o)$ is a countably infinite pointed metric space with separation at least one and diameter at most $B=B_p$. If a source vertex $x$ has color $i$, then $h_i(x)=0$, and $h(a(x))=h(x)$.*\n\n3.  *For every source vertex $x$ and every anchor $a$, $$d_p(x,a)\\ge R=R_p,\\qquad d_p(x,a(x))=R.$$ In particular, $d_p(x,o)\\ge R_p=30p$.*\n\n4.  *Every closed $d_p$-ball of radius $2L$ has at most $Q$ points.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-support",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T_0:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ satisfies $\\left\\|T_0\\right\\|\\le p$ and $$\\left\\|T_0\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<\\frac12\\qquad(a\\in A_p).$$ Then there are a finite set $S\\subseteq M_p$ containing $o$ and a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ such that $$\\label{eq:operator-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n T\\mathcal F(M_p)\\subseteq\\operatorname{span}\\{\\delta_j:j\\in S\\},\\qquad\n \\left\\|T\\right\\|\\le2p,\\\\\n \\left\\|T\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<1\\qquad(a\\in A_p).\n \\end{gathered}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-330.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-support",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T_0:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ satisfies $\\left\\|T_0\\right\\|\\le p$ and $$\\left\\|T_0\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<\\frac12\\qquad(a\\in A_p).$$ Then there are a finite set $S\\subseteq M_p$ containing $o$ and a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ such that $$\\label{eq:operator-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n T\\mathcal F(M_p)\\subseteq\\operatorname{span}\\{\\delta_j:j\\in S\\},\\qquad\n \\left\\|T\\right\\|\\le2p,\\\\\n \\left\\|T\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<1\\qquad(a\\in A_p).\n \\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-330.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T_0:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ satisfies $\\left\\|T_0\\right\\|\\le p$ and $$\\left\\|T_0\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<\\frac12\\qquad(a\\in A_p).$$ Then there are a finite set $S\\subseteq M_p$ containing $o$ and a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ such that $$\\label{eq:operator-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n T\\mathcal F(M_p)\\subseteq\\operatorname{span}\\{\\delta_j:j\\in S\\},\\qquad\n \\left\\|T\\right\\|\\le2p,\\\\\n \\left\\|T\\delta_a-\\delta_a\\right\\|<1\\qquad(a\\in A_p).\n \\end{gathered}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-measure-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\subseteq M_p$ be finite and contain $o$, and let $T:\\mathcal F(M_p)\\to\\mathcal F(M_p)$ be a bounded linear operator satisfying <a href=\"#eq:operator-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operator-bounds\">[eq:operator-bounds]</a>. There is a family $(\\mu_x)_{x\\in M_p}$ of signed measures on $S$, each of total mass one, such that $$\\label{eq:affine-pairing}\n \\int g\\,d\\mu_x=(T\\delta_x)(g-g(o))+g(o)$$ for every real Lipschitz function $g$ on $M_p$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-non-tree-bad-set",
        "exact_quote": "One may enlarge the set counted by $\\beta_m$ to include every root whose induced radius-$(n+1)$ neighborhood is not a tree. For fixed $n$, let $\\gamma_m$ be the fraction of roots with such a neighborhood and $\\widetilde\\beta_m$ the enlarged fraction. Then $\\beta_m\\le\\widetilde\\beta_m\\le\\beta_m+\\gamma_m$, while the local limit gives $\\gamma_m\\to0$. Thus <a href=\"#eq:bad-fraction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bad-fraction\">[eq:bad-fraction]</a> remains valid with $\\widetilde\\beta_m$ in place of $\\beta_m$, and the same successive choices of $n\\ge2$ and sufficiently large $m$ give $\\widetilde\\beta_m<1/(20V)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pointed Lipschitz functions** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a pointed metric space $(M,d,o)$, let $\operatorname{Lip}_0(M)$ be the Banach space of real Lipschitz functions vanishing at $o$, with the Lipschitz constant as norm.
- **Lipschitz-free space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its *Lipschitz-free space* is $$\mathcal F(M)=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{\delta_x:x\in M\} \subseteq\operatorname{Lip}_0(M)^*,\qquad \delta_x(f)=f(x).$$
- **Approximation property** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Banach space $X$ has the *approximation property* (AP) if for every norm-compact set $C\subseteq X$ and every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a bounded finite-rank linear operator $T:X\to X$ with $\sup_{u\in C}\left\\|Tu-u\right\\|<\varepsilon$.
- **Bounded approximation at a prescribed bound** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $\lambda\ge1$, it has $\lambda$-BAP if these operators can always be chosen with $\left\\|T\right\\|\le\lambda$.
- **BAP and MAP** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The bounded approximation property (BAP) means $\lambda$-BAP for some finite $\lambda$, and the metric approximation property (MAP) means $1$-BAP.
- **Proper metric space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A metric space is *proper* if its closed bounded balls are compact.
- **Capped color-distance labels** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For a source vertex $x\in G_m$, define $h(x)\in\{0,\ldots,D\}^k$ by $$h_i(x)=\min\bigl\{D,\operatorname{dist}_{G_m}(x,\{\text{vertices of color }i\})\bigr\} \qquad(1\le i\le k).$$
- **Label bump** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a source vertex $x$, define a function on all of $M_p$ by $$\label{eq:bump} b_x(t)=L\left(1-\frac{\left\\|h(x)-h(t)\right\\|_\infty}{K}\right)_+, \qquad s_+=\max\{s,0\}.$$
- **Matched walk** ([source](#sec:walks)): A nonbacktracking walk $x_0,e_1,x_1,\ldots,e_t,x_t$ in one graph $G_m$ is *matched* if there are states $j_0,\ldots,j_t\in S$ such that $(x_s,j_s)$ is compatible for every $s$ and $$d_p(j_{s-1},j_s)\le2L\qquad(1\le s\le t).$$
- **Continuation record** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For a good vertex $x$, an incidence $e\in\mathcal D(x)$, and a state $j\in S$, let $\mathcal L_x(e,j)$ be the set of positive integer lengths of matched walks starting through $e$ with initial state $j$. Define $$r_x(e,j)=\max\bigl(\{0\}\cup\mathcal L_x(e,j)\bigr).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-limit): Permutation exploration and finite-union probabilistic selection. -> Independent permutations and vertex colors admit a deterministic sequence with the required colored tree local limit.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-geometry): Weighted shortest paths and graph-walk counting. -> The weighted metric construction preserves separation, bounded diameter, label control, anchor distances and small-ball bounds.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coefficients): Sign tests and coordinate truncations. -> The coefficient map identifies a bounded separated free space with absolutely summable coefficients and controls coordinate truncations.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-support): Finite-dimensional range coordinates and evaluation approximation. -> A finite-rank operator can be perturbed to have a common finite evaluation support while retaining the required bounds.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-measure-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-measure-bounds): Affine mass correction and variation tests. -> Affine signed measures of total mass one encode the supported operator, with variation and bump-integral bounds.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-rare-walks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rare-walks): Necessary color memberships, tree independence and union bounds. -> The tree color estimate bounds the asymptotic fraction of roots admitting long matched walks.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-rem-non-tree-bad-set) / [proof](#stmt-rem-non-tree-bad-set): Local convergence of non-tree neighborhood frequencies. -> Roots with non-tree neighborhoods may be added to the bad set without changing the limiting estimate.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-exceptional-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-directions): Symmetric continuation-record inequalities. -> Compatible nearby states across an incidence force at least one exceptional direction.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-test): Opposing prescriptions, scalar extension and clipping. -> Opposing bump prescriptions extend to a bounded scalar Lipschitz test.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-test-2): Signed-measure pairings and uniform incidence averaging. -> An infinite bounded block has a finite anchor set that obstructs every finite-rank operator within the prescribed norm bound.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-cor-renorming) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coefficients-2): Isometric transport of the free-space norm. -> The free-space norm transfers to an equivalent real sequence-space norm with bounded approximation but a prescribed obstruction.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-9): Metric wedge retractions and finite block approximations. -> The metric wedge has compact-set finite-rank approximations without any uniform operator norm bound.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-rem-abstract-obstruction) / [proof](#stmt-rem-abstract-obstruction): Reuse of the source-bound metric and graph hypotheses. -> The anchor obstruction depends only on the listed metric, label, local-limit and incidence assumptions fixed before the operator.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-extension**: Finite scalar Lipschitz data extend while preserving the prescribed values, the Lipschitz bound, and the clipped range.
- **I-order**: The metric block, anchors, and deterministic graph sequence are fixed before the operator; the walk length and source graph are chosen after its support.
- **I-support**: Finite-rank approximation requires a perturbation before its range has a common finite evaluation support.
- **I-signed**: The affine representation uses signed measures with common total mass and bounded total variation; positivity is not required.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-uniform**: Use compact-set approximation to claim a uniform bound on approximating operator norms. -> The wedge has AP while every finite BAP bound fails.
- **A-support**: Treat a finite-rank range as already supported on finitely many evaluations. -> Finite dimension alone does not provide the support required by the matched-walk argument.
- **A-positive**: Replace the affine signed measures by probability measures. -> Arbitrary operator coefficients can have either sign, so positivity adds an unsupported restriction.
- **A-finite-certificate**: Use a finite scalar-extension computation to certify the entire AP-without-BAP example. -> The obstruction also requires the graph limit and operator-dependent successive choices.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite prescribed scalar data in the scalar-test proof admit an explicit clipped Lipschitz extension. The computation covers this finite subresult; the deterministic graph limit and infinite anchor obstruction remain outside its scope.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with cases of rational distance matrices d, height L, and prescribed values P.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each matrix is a finite metric; L is nonnegative; P is bounded and 1-Lipschitz.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational values of the clipped scalar extension, or REJECTED for invalid data.
# TIME: O(c*n^3) exact rational operations for c cases and n points per case.
# SPACE: O(c*n^2) rational input storage and O(c*n) output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq, exact rational arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid individual cases return REJECTED; malformed outer JSON exits nonzero.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpq


def rational(s):
    if not isinstance(s, str) or len(s) > 80:
        raise ValueError('rational string required')
    return fmpq(s)


def extend(case):
    try:
        matrix = [[rational(t) for t in row] for row in case['d']]
        n = len(matrix)
        if not 1 <= n <= 6 or any(len(row) != n for row in matrix):
            raise ValueError('matrix size')
        height = rational(case['L'])
        if height < 0:
            raise ValueError('height')
        for x in range(n):
            for y in range(n):
                if matrix[x][y] != matrix[y][x]:
                    raise ValueError('symmetry')
                if (x == y and matrix[x][y] != 0) or (x != y and matrix[x][y] <= 0):
                    raise ValueError('separation')
                for z in range(n):
                    if matrix[x][y] > matrix[x][z] + matrix[z][y]:
                        raise ValueError('triangle')
        prescribed = {}
        for key, value in case['P'].items():
            j = int(key)
            if key != str(j) or not 0 <= j < n:
                raise ValueError('point index')
            prescribed[j] = rational(value)
            if abs(prescribed[j]) > height:
                raise ValueError('range')
        for j in prescribed:
            for k in prescribed:
                if abs(prescribed[j] - prescribed[k]) > matrix[j][k]:
                    raise ValueError('data not Lipschitz')
        if not prescribed:
            answer = [fmpq(0) for _ in range(n)]
        else:
            answer = []
            for x in range(n):
                envelope = min(prescribed[j] + matrix[x][j] for j in prescribed)
                answer.append(max(-height, min(height, envelope)))
        return {'status': 'OK', 'values': [str(v) for v in answer]}
    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, IndexError, AttributeError, ZeroDivisionError):
        return {'status': 'REJECTED'}


payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
cases = payload['cases']
if not isinstance(cases, list) or not 1 <= len(cases) <= 20000:
    raise ValueError('case count')
print(json.dumps({'results': [extend(case) for case in cases]}, separators=(',', ':')))
```

          for k in prescribed:\n                if abs(prescribed[j] - prescribed[k]) > matrix[j][k]:\n                    raise ValueError('data not Lipschitz')\n        if not prescribed:\n            answer = [fmpq(0) for _ in range(n)]\n        else:\n            answer = []\n            for x in range(n):\n                envelope = min(prescribed[j] + matrix[x][j] for j in prescribed)\n                answer.append(max(-height, min(height, envelope)))\n        return {'status': 'OK', 'values': [str(v) for v in answer]}\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, IndexError, AttributeError, ZeroDivisionError):\n        return {'status': 'REJECTED'}\n\n\npayload = json.load(sys.stdin)\ncases = payload['cases']\nif not isinstance(cases, list) or not 1 <= len(cases) <= 20000:\n    raise ValueError('case count')\nprint(json.dumps({'results': [extend(case) for case in cases]}, separators=(',', ':')))\n",
    "test_vectors": [

        "expected": "VALID_EXTENSION",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The construction and operator obstruction are over the real scalars. A corresponding complex-scalar result is not established here. Blocked use: ['Transferring the obstruction to complex Lipschitz-free spaces without an additional argument.']
- **L2** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample is countable, uniformly discrete, unbounded and nonproper. It does not contradict metric approximation for free spaces over countable proper metric spaces. Blocked use: ['Using this example as a counterexample in the countable proper setting.']
- **L3** (approximation): Approximation on each norm-compact set permits the operator norm to depend on the compact set and the requested error; it does not supply a uniform bound. Blocked use: ['Treating AP as a uniform stability guarantee for finite-rank approximations.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): Each bounded block has bounded approximation at a larger norm bound while obstructing the prescribed smaller bound. Failure of BAP belongs to the wedge, not to each individual bounded block. Blocked use: ['Reporting that each bounded block fails every bounded approximation property.']
- **L5** (edge_case): The finite anchor obstruction requires an infinite block. A finite metric pair can obstruct prescribed-range projections while its free-space identity is still finite rank. Blocked use: ['Replacing the infinite block by a finite metric pair and claiming the same finite-rank identity obstruction.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The coefficient identification uses both bounded diameter and uniform separation. The coefficient argument cannot be applied directly to the unbounded wedge with a finite diameter constant. Blocked use: ['Dropping boundedness or uniform separation from the coefficient-isomorphism hypotheses.']
- **L7** (edge_case): The singleton pointed space gives zero coefficient and free spaces and must be handled separately from the nontrivial diameter regime. Blocked use: ['Assuming a nonzero free space for the singleton metric space.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The affine measures have total mass one but need not be positive. Their total variation, rather than probability normalization, controls exceptional losses. Blocked use: ['Replacing the signed measures by probability measures without justification.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The graph sequence, block and anchors precede the operator; the finite support precedes the walk length and the selected source graph. Local convergence is applied only after this support is fixed. Blocked use: ['Reversing the order of choices or assuming independence between the operator and the graph sequence.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): The local-limit proof selects realizations by positive probability and sufficiently large graph size. It supplies deterministic existence but no explicit stopping bound for selecting a graph that satisfies the eventual support-dependent rarity condition. Blocked use: ['Claiming a certified finite runtime for constructing the full infinite counterexample from the local-limit existence proof.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): Nonbacktracking is defined by reversed edge incidences, including loops and parallel edges. Repeated states and vertex revisits are permitted. Blocked use: ['Replacing the incidence convention by a simple-graph or self-avoiding-walk convention.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The finite scalar extension formula constructs a clipped Lipschitz function from finite consistent scalar data. Verifying that formula on finite inputs does not establish the graph-limit argument or the infinite Banach-space counterexample. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite scalar-extension harness as a certificate of the main approximation-property theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 330 (`lean/docs/330.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A uniformly discrete counterexample to bounded approximation in Lipschitz-free spaces](../../preprints/Failure-of-Bounded-Approximation-in-a-Lipschitz-Free-Space-over-a-Uniformly-Discrete-Metric-Space-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Kalton's question asks whether uniform discreteness of a metric space forces its Lipschitz-free space to have the bounded approximation property. The formalization constructs a countable metric space with all distinct points at distance at least one whose real Lipschitz-free space has the approximation property but fails every bounded approximation bound. The metric space is unbounded and not proper.
>
> The earlier quantitative renorming result for real $\ell_1$ is retained: for each integer $p\ge1$, an equivalent complete norm has the $2B_p$-bounded approximation property but fails the $p$-bounded approximation property, where $B_p=300p^2+150p+5$.

Comparator source `DiscreteLipschitzFree.lean` (5868 bytes, sha256 `af1c691c7cff4a059a7fee5b6993892c0331de0ba4f0c5ab3915b7beee435e70`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
# Real Lipschitz-free spaces and approximation properties
-/

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators ENNReal

namespace DiscreteFree

variable {M : Type*} [MetricSpace M]

def lipSubmodule (o : M) : Submodule ℝ (M → ℝ) where
  carrier := {f | f o = 0 ∧ ∃ C : ℝ, 0 ≤ C ∧
    ∀ x y, |f x - f y| ≤ C * dist x y}
  zero_mem' := by
    refine ⟨rfl, 0, le_rfl, ?_⟩
    intro x y
    simp
  add_mem' := by
    rintro f g ⟨hfo, C, hC, hf⟩ ⟨hgo, D, hD, hg⟩
    refine ⟨by simpa using congrArg₂ (· + ·) hfo hgo,
      C + D, add_nonneg hC hD, ?_⟩
    intro x y
    change |f x + g x - (f y + g y)| ≤ (C + D) * dist x y
    calc
      _ = |(f x - f y) + (g x - g y)| := by ring_nf
      _ ≤ |f x - f y| + |g x - g y| := abs_add_le _ _
      _ ≤ C * dist x y + D * dist x y := add_le_add (hf x y) (hg x y)
      _ = _ := by ring
  smul_mem' := by
    rintro c f ⟨hfo, C, hC, hf⟩
    refine ⟨by simp [hfo], |c| * C, mul_nonneg (abs_nonneg c) hC, ?_⟩
    intro x y
    change |c * f x - c * f y| ≤ (|c| * C) * dist x y
    calc
      _ = |c| * |f x - f y| := by rw [← mul_sub, abs_mul]
      _ ≤ |c| * (C * dist x y) := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (hf x y) (abs_nonneg c)
      _ = _ := by ring

def Lip0 (o : M) := ↥(lipSubmodule o)

namespace Lip0

variable {o : M}

instance : AddCommGroup (Lip0 o) := inferInstanceAs (AddCommGroup ↥(lipSubmodule o))
instance : Module ℝ (Lip0 o) := inferInstanceAs (Module ℝ ↥(lipSubmodule o))

instance : CoeFun (Lip0 o) (fun _ => M → ℝ) := ⟨fun f => f.val⟩

def slope (o : M) : Lip0 o →ₗ[ℝ] lp (fun _ : M × M => ℝ) ∞ where
  toFun f := ⟨fun z : M × M => (f z.1 - f z.2) / dist z.1 z.2, by
    obtain ⟨C, hC, hf⟩ := f.property.2
    apply memℓp_infty
    refine ⟨C, ?_⟩
    rintro r ⟨⟨x, y⟩, rfl⟩
    change ‖(f x - f y) / dist x y‖ ≤ C
    rw [Real.norm_eq_abs, abs_div, abs_of_nonneg dist_nonneg]
    by_cases hxy : x = y
    · simp [hxy, hC]
    · exact (div_le_iff₀ (dist_pos.mpr hxy)).2 (hf x y)⟩
  map_add' f g := by
    apply lp.ext
    funext z
    change ((f z.1 + g z.1) - (f z.2 + g z.2)) / dist z.1 z.2 =
      (f z.1 - f z.2) / dist z.1 z.2 + (g z.1 - g z.2) / dist z.1 z.2
    ring
  map_smul' c f := by
    apply lp.ext
    funext z
    change (c * f z.1 - c * f z.2) / dist z.1 z.2 =
      c * ((f z.1 - f z.2) / dist z.1 z.2)
    ring

instance (o : M) : NormedAddCommGroup (Lip0 o) :=
  NormedAddCommGroup.induced _ _ (slope o).toAddMonoidHom (by
    intro f g h
    apply Subtype.ext
    funext x
    have hx := congrArg (fun q : lp (fun _ : M × M => ℝ) ∞ => q (x, o)) h
    change (f x - f o) / dist x o = (g x - g o) / dist x o at hx
    rw [f.property.1, g.property.1, sub_zero, sub_zero] at hx
    by_cases hxo : x = o
    · rw [hxo, f.property.1, g.property.1]
    · exact (div_left_inj' (ne_of_gt (dist_pos.mpr hxo))).mp hx)

instance (o : M) : NormedSpace ℝ (Lip0 o) :=
  NormedSpace.induced ℝ _ _ (slope o)

end Lip0

def evaluation (o x : M) : (Lip0 o →L[ℝ] ℝ) :=
  LinearMap.mkContinuous
    ({ toFun := fun f : Lip0 o => f x
       map_add' := fun _ _ => rfl
       map_smul' := fun _ _ => rfl } : Lip0 o →ₗ[ℝ] ℝ)
    (dist x o) (fun f => by
      change ‖f x‖ ≤ dist x o * ‖f‖
      rw [mul_comm]
      have hx := lp.norm_apply_le_norm (by simp : (∞ : ℝ≥0∞) ≠ 0)
        (Lip0.slope o f) (x, o)
      change ‖(f x - f o) / dist x o‖ ≤ ‖f‖ at hx
      rw [f.property.1, sub_zero, norm_div, Real.norm_of_nonneg dist_nonneg] at hx
      by_cases hxo : x = o
      · rw [hxo, f.property.1, norm_zero, dist_self, mul_zero]
      · exact (div_le_iff₀ (dist_pos.mpr hxo)).mp hx)

def freeSubmodule (o : M) : Submodule ℝ ((Lip0 o →L[ℝ] ℝ)) :=
  (Submodule.span ℝ (Set.range (evaluation o))).topologicalClosure

def FreeSpace (o : M) := ↥(freeSubmodule o)

instance (o : M) : NormedAddCommGroup (FreeSpace o) :=
  inferInstanceAs (NormedAddCommGroup ↥(freeSubmodule o))

instance (o : M) : NormedSpace ℝ (FreeSpace o) :=
  inferInstanceAs (NormedSpace ℝ ↥(freeSubmodule o))

def delta (o x : M) : FreeSpace o :=
  ⟨evaluation o x, Submodule.le_topologicalClosure _
    (Submodule.subset_span ⟨x, rfl⟩)⟩

def HasAP (X : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace ℝ X] : Prop :=
  ∀ C : Set X, IsCompact C → ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
    ∃ T : X →L[ℝ] X, Module.Finite ℝ (LinearMap.range T.toLinearMap) ∧
      ∃ η : ℝ, η < ε ∧ ∀ u ∈ C, ‖T u - u‖ ≤ η

def HasBAP (X : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup X] [NormedSpace ℝ X] (Λ : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ C : Set X, IsCompact C → ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
    ∃ T : X →L[ℝ] X, Module.Finite ℝ (LinearMap.range T.toLinearMap) ∧
      ‖T‖ ≤ Λ ∧ ∃ η : ℝ, η < ε ∧ ∀ u ∈ C, ‖T u - u‖ ≤ η

def blockBound (p : ℕ) : ℝ := 300 * (p : ℝ)^2 + 150 * (p : ℝ) + 5

def BlockStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ p : ℕ, 1 ≤ p → ∃ (M : Type) (d : MetricSpace M) (o : M),
    letI := d
    Countable M ∧ (∀ x y : M, x ≠ y → 1 ≤ dist x y) ∧
      (∀ x y : M, dist x y ≤ blockBound p) ∧
      ∃ A : Finset M, o ∈ A ∧ ∀ T : FreeSpace o →L[ℝ] FreeSpace o,
        Module.Finite ℝ (LinearMap.range T.toLinearMap) → ‖T‖ ≤ (p : ℝ) →
          ∃ a ∈ A, (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ ‖T (delta o a) - delta o a‖

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (M : Type) (d : MetricSpace M) (o : M),
    letI := d
    Countable M ∧ (∀ x y : M, x ≠ y → 1 ≤ dist x y) ∧
      (¬ Bornology.IsBounded (Set.univ : Set M)) ∧
      (¬ ProperSpace M) ∧ HasAP (FreeSpace o) ∧
      ∀ Λ : ℝ, 1 ≤ Λ → ¬ HasBAP (FreeSpace o) Λ

theorem source_endpoints : BlockStatement ∧ MainStatement := by
  sorry

end DiscreteFree

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a countable uniformly discrete metric space whose real Lipschitz-free space has the approximation property but fails the bounded approximation property, answering Kalton's question negatively. The identity can be approximated on every compact set by finite-rank operators, but no uniform bound on their norms is possible.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
